# Recovery Workbench — AI engineering standards (v2)

This is the standards file the coding agent reads before every task, as defined in
`Recovery Workbench AI Assisted Development Approach v1` (section 3). It applies to the recovery
module of the ACE service and to anyone using an AI tool on this repository.

**Owner:** Solution architect with the TechX tech leads.
**Change control:** pull request, reviewed like code.
**Baseline:** document set v14, 16 November 2026. The implementation evidence from 10 November 2026
(`RWB-E1-042`, `RWB-TE-001`) is preserved; both are re-specified against this baseline.
**Source:** `requirement/legacy/08-ai-and-agents/recovery-workbench-ai-assisted-development-approach-v1.md`
and `requirement/legacy/11-delivery-and-operations/recovery-workbench-engineering-practices-v5-docx.md`.

## 1. The seven working rules

1. The person who merges a change owns it, whoever or whatever wrote it.
2. The agent works from files: the standards file, the story file and the plan file. Anything the
   agent needs is in one of the three.
3. The tests and the acceptance criteria exist before the code is generated.
4. Every line that reaches production is read by a person.
5. An AI error found by a reviewer is written into this file, so the agent is told not to repeat it.
6. No personal data or production data goes into a prompt, a test or a log.
7. The unit of work is one story. Work is merged at least once a working day.

## 2. Structure and stack

- The Workbench is the recovery module of the ACE service: one service, one repository, one front end.
- Back end: Spring Boot (Java 21), Spring JPA, Spring Batch, Spring Security with method security,
  Spring Session, Spring Cloud Azure, Spring Data REST, Spring HATEOAS and Quartz.
- Front end: React on Vite with Radix and TanStack Query. Front-end types are generated from the
  back end.
- Database: PostgreSQL, schema `recovery` in the ACE database, referenced only as
  `${recoverySchema}` and fixed to `recovery` in every environment (D-RCV-28). Schema changes use
  versioned migrations, one migration per story, forward-only. Files are named
  `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`, where `<seq>` is a four-digit sequence taken in merge order and line 1
  is `-- Story: <story key>`.
- Domain driven design, RESTful API, end-to-end type safety and validation.
- Cases are modelled in CMMN 1.1 terms: case, stage, milestone, task, sentry, event listener and
  case file item. From Epic 3 the case lifecycle runs on Temporal; case data stays in the recovery
  schema (D-RCV-36, ADR-011).
- Rules are versioned data, authored as Drools XLS decision-table packages and evaluated by embedded
  Drools (D-RCV-36, ADR-012).
- Hosting: the ACE namespace on the shared ACE AKS clusters; Temporal is self-hosted in the same
  namespace.

## 3. The agent must

1. Put recovery code in the recovery module folder only. Change the common or collection module only
   where the story file names it.
2. Read every threshold, limit, timer, queue, role, template and route from its versioned reference
   table. Never write one as a literal in code.
3. Put a Spring Security method check on every endpoint, naming the capability area and the operation.
4. Write a decision record entry for every change to a case and every decision, with the rule ID and
   version used.
5. Use the names in the business glossary for classes, fields, tables and endpoints.
6. Return each validation message from the message table by its rule ID.
7. Make database changes by migration script only, one script per story. Name it
   `V<seq>__<story>_<name>.sql`; line 1 is `-- Story: <story key>`. A corrective script takes the
   next sequence, keeps the original story key and adds line 2 `-- Fix: <reason>` (D-RCV-28).
8. Use Spring Batch and Quartz for every Epic 1...

-- Reference version: One version of a reference or rule table, with its author, approver, checks and change checklist.
CREATE TABLE reference_version (
    id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    table_code varchar(30) NOT NULL,
    version_no integer NOT NULL,
    version_label varchar(20),
    valid_from date NOT NULL,
    valid_to date,
    status varchar(15) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'VALIDATED',
    author_user_id uuid NOT NULL,
    approver_user_id uuid,
    approved_at timestamptz,
    row_count integer NOT NULL,
    content_hash char(64) NOT NULL,
    load_id uuid NOT NULL,
    dry_run_counts jsonb,
    rollback_reason varchar(400),
    change_request_ref varchar(30),
    impact_summary varchar(1000),
    test_result varchar(10),
    rollback_plan varchar(1000),
    verified_by uuid,
    verified_at timestamptz,
    verify_note varchar(1000),
    is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    state varchar(40),
    version integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    created_by uuid,
    updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_by uuid,
    CONSTRAINT uk_reference_version UNIQUE (table_code, version_no)
);

-- Reference row: One row of a reference table version held as data.
CREATE TABLE reference_row (
    id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    version_id uuid NOT NULL,
    row_key varchar(200) NOT NULL,
    row_data jsonb NOT NULL,
    is_active boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
    state varchar(40),
    version integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 1,
    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    created_by uuid,
    updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_by uuid,
    CONSTRAINT uk_reference_row UNIQUE (row_key)
);

-- Rule test case: A test case of the rule table test harness, with its expected and actual result.
CREATE TABLE rule_test_case (
    id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    test_case_id varchar(20) NOT NULL,
    route_set varchar(15) NOT NULL,

# Data model: RWB-E1-042

Two persisted tables in schema `recovery`, three added columns, one migration, one catalog file and one grants file. The catalog file is the single source of parameter metadata; `parameter_catalog` is its persisted mirror for the foreign key, Physical Schema v6 and SQL consumers, and **no Java code reads it** (SPEC-005, A-29). No field of this story holds customer data; author, approver and audit user IDs are staff identifiers and are treated as personal (SPEC-027).

# Recovery Workbench, local build of the ACE recovery module.
# Profile "local" (application-local.yaml) points at the docker compose stack.
# SIT/UAT/PROD values come from the ACE deployment and Key Vault; nothing secret lives here.

spring:
  application:
    name: ace-recovery
  threads:
    virtual:
      enabled: true
  datasource:
    url: ${RWB_DB_URL:jdbc:postgresql://localhost:5433/ace}
    username: ${RWB_DB_USER:recovery_svc}
    password: ${RECOVERY_SVC_PASSWORD:}
    hikari:
      maximum-pool-size: 50          # NFR: 180 concurrent users, pool 50
      connection-init-sql: SET TIME ZONE 'Asia/Bangkok'
  jpa:
    open-in-view: false              # BS-3.6: no lazy loading outside a transaction

      ddl-auto: none
    properties:
      hibernate.default_schema: ${rwb.schema}
      hibernate.jdbc.time_zone: Asia/Bangkok
  flyway:

    include-message: never

```text
package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.platform.api;

import java.util.Map;

/** Thrown by any recovery module to answer with a platform {@link ApiError}. */
public class ApiProblemException extends RuntimeException {

	private final ApiError error;
	private final Map<String, String> placeholders;

	public ApiProblemException(ApiError error, Map<String, String> placeholders, Throwable cause) {
		super(error.messageCode(), cause);
		this.error = error;
		this.placeholders = Map.copyOf(placeholders);
	}

	public ApiError error() {
		return error;
	}

	public Map<String, String> placeholders() {
		return placeholders;
	}
}

```

# Recovery Workbench �?" testing standards

**Context ID:** `CTX-TESTING-RWB-V2`
**Version:** 2.0, 16 November 2026
**Source:** Test Strategy v3, Test Cases v7, Regression Suite v6, Test Data Plan v3, Engineering
Practices v5, D-RCV-06/07/28/40/43/44.

## 1. Test levels and tools

| Level | Tool | Scope | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| Unit (backend) | JUnit 5, AssertJ, Mockito | Classes and services | 80% lines/branches on recovery packages |
| Unit (frontend) | Vitest, Testing Library | Components, hooks, utils | 80% on `src/modules/recovery/**` |
| Integration (backend) | Spring Boot Test, Testcontainers PostgreSQL (`recovery-it`) | Repository, service, migration, view | Pass before every MR that touches a migration or Flyway config |
| Contract | Spring Cloud Contract / OpenAPI schema test | API request/response | Pass on every API change |
| BDD / functional | Cucumber, Playwright | Journeys | Pass in SIT |
| API | REST Assured / Playwright API | Endpoints, security, errors | Pass in SIT |
| Performance | `RWB-TE-009` harness | p95, throughput, soak | Pass before release |
| Accessibility | axe + Playwright (`RWB-TE-010`) | All recovery screens | No serious/critical issue |
| Security | PDPA lint, dependency scan, ZAP baseline | Code, logs, images | Pass on every MR |
| Migration | `recoveryIntegrationTest` + lint | Naming, sequence, schema, apply/rollback | Pass before merge |
| View conformance | SQL test | 30 views compile, no FK to `public`, grants | Pass before merge |

## 2. Test data

- Synthetic data only, generated by the synthetic generator (RWB-E1-065).
- SIT uses masked data (RWB-TE-004); UAT uses masked data; no production data anywhere.
- Every test fixture carries a story key and a purpose; no real customer name, ID, phone or account.
- Personal data fields are classified; a test that needs one uses a generated value and asserts masking.
- Test databases are per-class or per-suite; no shared mutable database.
- Testcontainers PostgreSQL 16; `@Tag("recovery-it")`; fail (not skip) when Docker is absent in the
  recovery integration task (D-RCV-28 (3)).

| Engineering enabler | One test per business rule and test case in the story |
| Migration story | Apply on empty, apply on baseline, re-run, negative cases, lint |
| View stor

```gherkin
Feature: Reference data service with versioned tables

  Background:
    Given the business date is 2026-11-25
    Given the table "EXPOSURE_BAND" has version 3 approved with valid from 2026-11-30 and bands "E1" to "E4"

```text
## 12. Contract

**POST /api/recovery/v1/admin/reference-loads** (P4 endpoint; calls this story's interface)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Request | multipart/form-data: `file` (CSV or XLSX, one sheet), `table` (a LOV-REF-TABLE code), `validFrom` (date) |
| Response | 201 `ReferenceLoad`: `id`, `table`, `validFrom`, `status` (VALIDATED), `rows` |
| Errors | 401 no session (MSG-P4-001); 403 no Reference data C (MSG-P4-002); 409 an approved version of the table already has this valid from (MSG-P3-027); 422 a check failed (MSG-P3-027) or the file has no rows (MSG-GEN-001); 503 the store is unavailable |
| Authorization | Reference data, right C |
| Idempotency | Table code, valid from and content hash. A repeat returns the existing load and writes nothing |

**POST /api/recovery/v1/admin/reference-loads/{loadId}/approve** (P4 endpoint)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Request | Path `loadId` (uuid). No body |
| Response | 200 `ReferenceLoad` with `status` APPROVED |
| Errors | 401; 403 no Reference data A (MSG-P4-002); 404 unknown load (MSG-P4-003); 409 the approver is the author (MSG-P7-002) or the version changed since it was read (MSG-GEN-004); 503 |
| Authorization | Reference data, right A |
| Idempotency | A second approval of an APPROVED version returns 200 with the same body and changes nothing |

**GET /api/recovery/v1/admin/lists/{listCode}** (P4 endpoint) reads a list with its versions (`ListOfValues`). Reference data R. Used by AC1 to read version 3.

}

    ReferenceVersion versionInForce(String tableCode, LocalDate businessDate);
    List<ReferenceVersion> versions(String tableCode);
}

public record ReferenceVersion(UUID id, String tableCode, int versionNo, LocalDate validFrom,
                               LocalDate validTo, String status, UUID authorUserId,
                               UUID approverUserId, Instant approvedAt, int rowCount, String contentHash) {}

## 16. Test cases

| TC-E1-043-10 | AC10, BR5 | System | DOCUMENT_TYPE file with a header only |
| TC-E1-043-11 | Access | Access | RW-CASEOWN (Reference data R only) calls the load endpoint; refused with MSG-P4-002 |

The full test, with its Given, When and Then, is in Recovery Workbench Test Cases v7.xlsx, tab Story test cases, under the same test case ID.

## 17. Guardrails

- No business value in code: table codes, columns, bands, periods and limits are rows; the code holds only the versioning behavior.
- No personal data in logs, tests, workflow history or prompts: log lines carry table codes and version numbers only, and test files use synthetic codes.
- No read of another module's tables; ACE data only through recovery.v_ace_* views: `lov_value` is read through the P4 published interface, and P4 reaches the registry only through `ReferenceVersionRegistry`.
- A version row is never updated in place except for its status, approver and valid to; a correction is a new version.
- The cache never serves a version that is not APPROVED.
- No rule table is held, loaded or versioned here: rule packages are Drools packages of RWB-E1-072 and RWB-E1-073, and LOV-REF-TABLE carries no rule table code.

```text
switch ($Command) {
  "up" {
    $composeArgs = @("up", "-d", "--wait")
    if ($App) { $composeArgs = @("--profile", "app") + $composeArgs + @("--build") }
    Invoke-Compose $composeArgs
    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "Postgres      localhost:$($env:POSTGRES_HOST_PORT) (database ace)"
    Write-Host "Temporal      localhost:7233   UI http://localhost:8233"
    Write-Host "Azurite Blob  http://localhost:10000"
    Write-Host "Mock Entra ID http://localhost:8090/entra/.well-known/openid-configuration"
    if ($App) { Write-Host "Backend       http://localhost:8080   Frontend http://localhost:5173" }
    else { Write-Host "Next: ./scripts/dev.ps1 backend   and   ./scripts/dev.ps1 frontend" }
  }
  "down" { Invoke-Compose @("--profile", "app", "down") }
  "reset" { Invoke-Compose @("--profile", "app", "down", "-v") }
  "logs" { Invoke-Compose @("--profile", "app", "logs", "-f", "--tail", "100") }
  "status" {
    Invoke-Compose @("--profile", "app", "ps")
    try { Invoke-RestMethod http://localhost:8080/actuator/health | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 4 } catch { Write-Host "Backend not reachable on :8080" }
  }
  "backend" {
    $env:SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE = "local"
    Invoke-Gradle @("bootRun")
  }
  "frontend" { Invoke-Npm @("run", "dev") }
  "test" {
    Invoke-Gradle @("test")
    Invoke-Npm @("run", "check")
  }
  "it" { Invoke-Gradle @("recoveryIntegrationTest") }
  "e2e" {
    Invoke-Gradle @("e2eTest")
    Invoke-Npm @("run", "e2e")
  }
}

```

```text
## 3. Key model (generated, `casewf.config.api`)

`ParameterKey<T>` is immutable and carries every catalog column. Constant names are the key upper-cased with `.` and `-` replaced by `_` (`CASE_ENTRY_DPD`, `RULES_TESTCASES_MIN`).

| Accessor | Type | From the catalog | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `name()` | `String` | `key` | |
| `kind()` | `Kind` (`BUSINESS`, `ENVIRONMENT`) | `kind` | |
| `type()` | `Class<T>` | `value_type` | INTEGER `Integer`, DECIMAL `BigDecimal`, BOOLEAN `Boolean`, DATE `LocalDate`, TIME `LocalTime`, DURATION `Duration`, CODE_LIST `List<String>` (unchecked cast at the single construction point), STRING/CODE/JSON `String` |
| `unit()` | `String` | `unit` | empty when absent |
| `min()`, `max()` | `String` | `min_value`, `max_value` | raw text, empty when absent; compared after conversion |
| `defaultValue()` | `String` | `default_value` | raw text, `null` when absent; converted by `ParameterValues` |
| `scope()` | `Scope` (`GLOBAL`, `PRODUCT`) | `scope` | |
| `required()` | `boolean` | `required` | |
| `ownerRole()` | `String` | `owner_role` | |
| `environments()` | `List<String>` | `environments` | immutable, in file order |
| `usedBy()` | `List<String>` | `used_by` | split on `;` and trimmed |
| `secret()` | `boolean` | `secret` | |
| `valueSource()` | `ValueSource` (`TABLE`, `ENV`, `SECRET`) | `value_source` | generated only, not a DB column |
| `envProperty()` | `String` | `env_property` | `null` when absent |
| `description()` | `String` | `description` | |

`static <T> ParameterKey<T> of(String name, Class<T> type)` builds an ad-hoc key with no metadata, for fixture modules and the completeness check's `ParameterKey.of` rule. Production code reads only the generated constants.

`ParameterKeys` (generated) adds `static List<ParameterKey<?>> all()` (file order, immutable) and `static <T> Optional<ParameterKey<T>> byName(String key, Class<T> type)` (empty when absent or the type differs).

**One converter.** `ParameterValues.convert(ParameterKey<?>, String)` in `casewf.config` turns catalog text into the typed value and is the only place conversion lives: `ParameterServiceImpl`, `ParameterLoadValidator` and `ParameterStartupCheck` all call it, so the load check and the read path cannot disagree about a value.

## 4. Tables

package th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.platform.internal;

import java.io.IOException;
import java.net.URI;
import java.util.Map;

import io.micrometer.tracing.Tracer;
import jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletResponse;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.ObjectProvider;
import org.springframework.http.HttpStatus;
import org.springframework.http.MediaType;
import org.springframework.http.ProblemDetail;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
import tools.jackson.databind.json.JsonMapper;

import th.co.scb.ace.demo.recovery.platform.api.ApiError;

/** Builds API Specification v4 {@code Problem} bodies, in controllers and in the security filters. */
@Component
class ProblemFactory {

	private final ObjectProvider<Tracer> tracer;
	private final JsonMapper json;

	ProblemFactory(ObjectProvider<Tracer> tracer, JsonMapper json) {
		this.tracer = tracer;
		this.json = json;
	}

	ProblemDetail problem(ApiError error, Map<String, String> placeholders) {
		HttpStatus status = HttpStatus.valueOf(error.status());
		ProblemDetail p = ProblemDetail.forStatusAndDetail(status, error.english(placeholders));
		p.setType(URI.create("urn:rwb:problem:" + error.messageCode()));
		p.setTitle(status.getReasonPhrase());
		p.setProperty("messageCode", error.messageCode());
		p.setProperty("messageTh", error.thai(placeholders));
		p.setProperty("traceId", traceId());
		return p;
	}

	void write(HttpServletResponse response, ApiError error, Map<String, String> placeholders) throws IOException {
		response.setStatus(error.status());
		response.setContentType(MediaType.APPLICATION_PROBLEM_JSON_VALUE);
		response.setCharacterEncoding("UTF-8");
		json.writeValue(response.getOutputStream(), problem(error, placeholders));
	}

	private String traceId() {
		Tracer t = tracer.getIfAvailable();
		return t != null && t.currentSpan() != null ? t.currentSpan().context().traceId() : null;
	}
}

```
